ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u65	Thank you very much, Minister. - REAL DECRETO-LEY 6/2020 OF 10 MARCH, BY WHICH CERTAIN EMERGENCY MEASURES IN THE ECONOMIC AREA AND FOR THE PROTECTION OF PUBLIC HEALTH ARE ADOPTED. [[File number 130/000007]] - REAL DECRETO-LEY 7/2020 OF 12 MARCH, BY WHICH EMERGENCY MEASURES ARE TAKEN TO RESPONSIBLE FOR THE ECONOMIC IMPACT OF COVID-19. [[File number 130/000008]] - REAL DECRETO-LEY 8/2020, OF 17 MARCH, OF EXTRAORDINARY EMERGENCY MEASURES TO BE FRONT OF THE ECONOMIC AND SOCIAL IMPACT OF COVID-19. [[File number 130/000009]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u66	We turn to the following item on the agenda: Validation or repeal of Royal Decree-Law 6/2020 of 10 March, adopting certain urgent measures in the economic field and for the protection of public health, which, as agreed by the Board of Spokespersons, will be discussed jointly with Royal Decree-Law 7/2020 of 12 March, adopting urgent measures to respond to the economic impact of COVID-19, and Royal Decree-Law 8/2020 of 17 March, on extraordinary urgent measures to address the economic and social impact of COVID-19. To present the three royal decree-laws, I have the floor, on behalf of the Government, the third Vice-President of the Government and Minister for Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u67	Thank you very much, President. Good afternoon, everyone. I am addressing the House today in such extraordinary circumstances to request the support of all groups for three royal decree-laws adopted in the exceptional context of the fight against COVID-19. Before we begin, I would like, as others have done before from the podium, to send a message of affection and support to all the people affected by the disease, to those who are in the hospital, to those who keep quarantine at home, in general to all the citizens of our country who are going through very difficult times because they have seen their way of life so profoundly altered. I would also like to accompany or join all those who have previously expressed their support, sincere gratitude, appreciation for those who are allowing our country's daily work, our economy, to continue to function; not only medical personnel, but those who work in supermarkets, shops, distributors, transporters, farmers, those who keep our cities clean, who allow us today to have this session in the Chamber. All those who are making Spain continue to function are those who are going to allow when this exceptional situation passes we can recover the normal pace of our lives and get out of this health, social and economic emergency as best possible, as quickly as possible. Since the disease of COVID-19 began to expand around the world and, above all, since it was declared a pandemic, the Government has continued to follow the situation minute by minute in order to be able to act quickly and effectively to deal with the different circumstances that have been occurring. In terms of health, we have taken the toughest containment measures in our environment - as the Minister of Health has also said today - and the same force with which we have acted to contain the spread of the virus marks the response we are giving at the economic and social levels. Thus, in parallel with health measures, we have adopted a set of economic and social measures that address three clear priorities: combating the epidemic, strengthening health services and research; supporting workers, families, the self-employed, ultimately all people in our country, particularly the most vulnerable, and supporting economic activity with liquidity and flexibility measures, easing the costs of companies to maintain a minimum of activity despite the intense slowdown in our country, so that we have a basis on which to build economic recovery as soon as we put the health crisis behind us. These are measures that are fully aligned with what countries in our environment are doing and with what international agencies are telling us. They are measures that accompany the decisions and recommendations of the health authorities, giving priority to the protection of the health and safety of people and trying to minimise, at the same time, the risk that the economic slowdown will have a structural impact, especially in terms of employment, which will put a burden on economic and social recovery when the exceptional situation passes. Moreover, our action is marked by a constant dialogue with the economic and social actors of our country, with the autonomous communities, the European authorities and the rest of the governments of our environment. And this is the context, ladies and gentlemen, in which today we bring to validation three royal decrees laws, adopted in just one week, which I am going to explain, not without pointing out that the Government is working on other complementary measures that may be necessary in the coming weeks, that is, the Government will continue to take, in the economic and social sphere, as the President of the Government said a week ago in this very House, as many measures as are necessary, how and when are necessary. Firstly, I should like to refer to health measures. The Royal Decree-Law of 10 March has already adopted a first measure to enable the State to carry out the centralized supply not only of medicines and health products, but also of other essential products for the protection of health, especially of our medical professionals, by amending the Organic Law on Special Measures in Public Health of 1986. As the Minister of Health said today, EUR 432 million in health supplies have been purchased under this measure. But it was, above all, the Royal Decree-Law of 12 March that significantly strengthened the funding of the health system. The resources of the Ministry of Health were strengthened by EUR 1 billion through the Contingency Fund to meet the extraordinary costs generated, so that health needs can be adequately met. EUR 28 billion is advanced from the payments to the autonomous communities, which have the first competence in this area, so that they can meet the immediate needs arising from this situation in their health systems. The Ministry of Finance has already started making payments for 1434 million euros this Monday, and the rest will pay it on 17 April. The Government is also empowered to regulate the prices of certain products necessary for health protection by authorising the Interministerial Committee on Prices for Medicinal Products to fix, in the event of an exceptional situation, the maximum amount of sale to the public of certain medicinal products and products. Finally, the Royal Decree-Law of 17 March introduced research support measures to promote knowledge of the disease to develop medicines and vaccines that help contain the impact of future outbreaks. In particular, the management requirements for both staff and budget management have been made more flexible in order to speed up the work of research centres. Extraordinary appropriations have been authorized to provide the Higher Council for Scientific Research, the CSIC, and the Carlos III Institute of Health with the necessary resources to meet the scientific and research challenges arising from the current health emergency. Voluntary release and confined use activities with genetically modified organisms have also been authorized provided that they are intended or proven to be useful in preventing, combating or containing the epidemic. And the technical provisions fund associated with the Cervera R+D+i Network has been modified to allow the financing of SME and medium-sized venture capital projects through loans managed by the CDTI, the Centre for Industrial Technological Development. A total of EUR 500 million is available for an aid line. Ladies and gentlemen, this is, above all, a health crisis and the Government will therefore spare no effort or resources to enable our National Health System to cope with this emergency. However, it cannot be seen that the crisis has transcended the merely health-care level and is clearly affecting the global economy. At first, when the COVID-19 was still confined in China, the economic impact came through the global value chains, as a result of the reduction in staffing and production capacity at one of the main international logistics centres in Hubei province. Subsequently, with the spread of the virus to other countries, difficulties in supply chains were accentuated and export demand was also affected, particularly in the tourism sector. The same was true of investment decisions by international actors in an environment of high uncertainty. Beyond the fall in external demand, the very containment measures that individual countries have been taking in recent weeks also have a direct impact on demand and domestic economic activity. The closure of schools, the suspension of public events and the ban on flights affect many sectors of the economy, particularly tourism and related services, transport, education or culture. With the strengthening of containment measures and even more with the declaration of the state of alarm and the temporary limitation of the free movement of citizens, the disruption of economic activity has become widespread. All this has occurred in a context of high volatility in the financial markets, the most obvious reflection of which is the extreme volatility of the stock exchange, turbulence in the commodity markets and tensions in the debt markets. All these circumstances, ladies and gentlemen, result in a joint disturbance of demand and supply for the Spanish economy that will affect the income of businesses and self-employed, generating liquidity tensions that could lead to a significant and more structural loss of employment if urgent stabilisation measures are not taken. In view of this development, increasing from the impact of the crisis of COVID-19 on the economy, we have resolutely adopted proportionate and effective measures that respond both to shocks on the supply side and on the demand side, paying special attention to people, families, self-employed people, workers, to help them overcome the consequences of the crisis in the best possible way, supporting citizens, especially the most vulnerable, at this time of difficulty. In the Royal Decree-Law of 10 March, in view of the first containment measures, it was decided to include periods of isolation or contagion of private sector workers as a result of the COVID-19 in a situation assimilated to the job loss due to an accident at work for the purpose of the temporary disability benefit of the social security system. The aim was to provide security and tranquillity for workers and self-employed persons affected by the obligations of isolation. This treatment was extended two days later to staff in the public service. In addition, the Royal Decree-Law of 12 March made social decisions to accompany the closure of schools. In particular, in order to prevent the risk of children's feeding, the Autonomous Communities were provided with €25 million to ensure, through food grants, the basic right to food for children in vulnerable situations affected by the closure of schools. In the development of this royal decree-law, in yesterday's Council of Ministers we agreed on the distribution of these credits, which will be granted through direct economic aid or the provision of direct food distribution to families who benefited from a scholarship or food aid and have been affected by the closure of educational establishments. The administrations affected by the reinforced containment measures were also empowered to modify the school timetable so that they could order the course in a flexible manner. These first social measures were strongly reinforced by those included in the Royal Decree-Law of 17 March, which provides for a moratorium on the payment of mortgage loans for vulnerable groups. As you all know, the Government is also working to provide solutions to those who find it difficult to pay their rent, taking into account the specificities of a market in which public entities, large housing holders, other professionals and also people who depend on rent for their monthly expenses. In addition to this moratorium, measures were adopted to facilitate the efforts of the beneficiaries of the unemployment benefit and the benefit for over 52 years of age. In addition to these measures, the Royal Decree-Law significantly strengthened the financial resources to support the most vulnerable groups in this exceptional situation, on the one hand, by creating a State programme, financed with 300 million euros, to guarantee the home care of dependent persons, which will be managed by the Ministry of Social Rights and Agenda 2030. Yesterday, the Council of Ministers also agreed on the criteria for the distribution of this extraordinary social fund, which will be based on population, dispersion, dependent population, surface area and insularity. With these amounts, the autonomous communities will be better prepared to strengthen residential neighbourhood services, especially for older persons, as well as tele-assistance arrangements, home rehabilitation or the strengthening of social services staffing. In addition, local corporations were allowed to use the 2019 surplus to finance expenditures on social services and social promotion. In this way, another EUR 300 million has been mobilised to enable the nearest administration, the local one, to respond immediately to the needs of the most vulnerable people and groups at this time, of those who may not be covered by the measures taken in the field of work. The Royal Decree-Law also prohibited the cutting of basic supplies of electricity, water and gas, as well as telecommunications, to vulnerable groups and extended the definition of protected subjects; the automatic extension until 15 September of the electric social bond was decreed; the review of the maximum selling prices of liquefied petroleum gases, i.e. butane candy, was suspended for six months; and the statutory deadline for citizens to make returns, a consumer protection measure, was also suspended. Beyond supporting citizens in the field of their private life, we have also taken measures at the labour level to protect jobs and minimise the loss of employment during this short-term situation, always guaranteeing the safety of workers who are making Spain stop. Thus, together with the decision which it pointed out to them before considering the periods of isolation or contagion of workers as an accident at work so that they can access the economic benefit due to temporary incapacity, by the Royal Decree-Law of 17 March, new measures were adopted to make working conditions more flexible, so that workers can reconcile their work obligations with the care of children, older or dependent, facilitating the right to adaptation of working hours as a less adverse mechanism for both the worker and the company itself; favouring non-personal work where possible as a means of maintaining productive activity, also maximizing the impact of containment measures on the prevention of contagion; simplifying the procedures of the temporary regulation of employment files, ERTEs, without diminishing the guarantees and legal certainty for both companies and workers. Ensuring that all workers affected by these types of cases as a result of the COVID-19 have access to the relevant benefits, even if they do not meet the minimum contribution periods and do not compute them for the purposes of consuming the maximum collection periods, is one of the elements of this safety net for workers, for all citizens, which we are establishing. Facilitating ERTE is a measure that responds to the express and shared request of the social partners and is a very important element because it implies that the State takes charge of the wage costs during this situation. Companies making use of this tool, in particular SMEs, will also be exempted from the payment of social contributions. In this way, we make it easier for the adjustment of employment to be transitory, avoiding the closure of businesses and dismissals that can be more structural, so that the impact does not go beyond what this health crisis lasts. And, above all, as I said a moment ago, we provide a safety net for the affected workers. We are now working with the social partners to coordinate the criteria for the application of ERTs in these exceptional situations throughout the national territory and the Ministry of Labour is taking a number of complementary measures to clarify that positive administrative silence is applied in the ERTs by force majeure; also measures to speed up the processing and payment of unemployment benefit to be received by the workers concerned, as well as measures to ensure that employment is maintained in those companies which deal with temporary adjustments in capacity and employment, benefiting from this ease of flexibility of ERTs. In addition to supporting dependent workers, a large block of the measures included in the actual decree-laws that we are debating today is addressed to self-employed workers, who are in a situation of fragility at the present juncture, which this Government is very sensitive to. That is why we have acted on three fronts of special relevance to ensure the maintenance of their activity. First, we postpone the payment of all taxes for a period of six months, upon request and with an interest rate subsidy. This includes both VAT quotas and IRPF deductions and other tax figures that apply to them, which will mean an injection of liquidity in the economy as a whole of around EUR 14 billion. Secondly, we have taken steps to reduce their costs and guarantee them minimum income if they suffer a significant fall in income or have to cease their activity. In particular, all self-employed workers, irrespective of their legal form, who are required to close their business or who suffer a fall in their turnover of 75 % will receive an extraordinary termination benefit equivalent to 70 % of the regulatory base. As the Minister for Inclusion, Social Security and Migration pointed out a few days ago, this amounts to a minimum of EUR 661. This measure also implies that Social Security will assume the payment of contributions from those who suffer a significant fall in their billing, as was already planned for those who requested the cessation of activity. This measure, ladies and gentlemen, therefore goes far beyond a mere abolition of quotas, since it means that self-employed people who run out of income, while this situation lasts, will not only not pay social contributions, but will also receive a benefit, and it will not only be those who enter zero, but also those who can maintain their activity will see that their billing falls by more than 75%. Similarly, self-employed persons with their dependent employees who are obliged to make a temporary employment regulation file will not have to pay their employees' benefits and social security contributions, which will be borne by the State, and self-employed persons who make fixed, discontinuous contracts between February and June in the tourism, trade and hospitality sectors, linked to tourism activity, will also have bonuses in the payment to social security. The third front for the benefit of the self-employed in which we have taken measures seeks to ensure liquidity and investment in this group. That is why they will be able to benefit from the liquidity guarantee measures of up to EUR 100 billion approved by the Government, so that they can cover their circulation, pay their bills or meet other concepts, thus maintaining their activity and employment. In this regard, yesterday the Council of Ministers approved a first tranche of up to EUR 20 billion, half of which will be reserved for self-employed people and SMEs. The guarantees shall be retroactive and shall guarantee 80 % of the new loans and business renewals requested by self-employed and SMEs. For the rest of the companies, they will cover 70 % of the new loan and 60 % of the renovations. The term of validity shall be equal to that of the loan, for a maximum period of five years. In addition, the self-employed will be able to apply for loans to finance their investment projects through the existing lines of the Official Credit Institute, which has been reinforced with an additional EUR 10 billion, and those of the tourism sector also the ICO specific line of EUR 400 million, which was provided to specifically cover this sector. And both the self-employed and the SMEs that need to make improvements in their computer equipment in order, for example, to be able to facilitate teleworking, will have the support of the Accelera programme, of Red.es, which has been launched with 250 million euros. We have put in place other measures which also benefit these groups, such as extending the time limit for responding to the requirements of the Tax Agency and regulating the suspension of public contracts. In short, they are very relevant, very powerful initiatives that can be complemented, of course, by new measures if necessary, depending on the needs that are being identified in our timely, daily, continuous follow-up of the situation. With regard to strengthening the health sector and the safety net for people, the Government's third objective is to support the business fabric of our country. As I said, containing the spread of the virus is our primary objective, but we must not forget that we must prevent the health crisis from becoming a more lasting economic crisis. We need to make this situation as short and as painful as possible, and that requires maintaining the business fabric and minimizing job destruction, thus preserving a basis on which to reactivate the economy, once we leave these difficult weeks behind. Thus, companies in general, particularly SMEs, can also benefit from the measures I have already mentioned, such as State guarantees, ICO credit lines, deferred tax payments, suspension of public procurement and the flexibility of ERTs. All this will mean an important oxygen balcony for many businesses. They will also benefit from the strengthening of the State-owned insurance cover for exporting companies through the Spanish Export Credit Insurance Company, Cesce, for up to EUR 2 billion, for circular financing operations, and from other measures, such as the postponement of the repayment of loans from the General Secretariat of Industry and the Small and Medium-sized Company, the possibility of commercial companies adapting their activities to the current circumstances, with flexibility for the development of meetings of government bodies, the formulation of accounts or holding of shareholders' boards, and also the flexibility of the deadlines for the declaration of competition. In the field of air transport, the rule governing the use of slots for the coming seasons has been suspended, so that airlines will not be penalised in the future for having reduced their flights in the current circumstances. Apart from these three specific areas: strengthening of the health system, safety net for people and support for the productive fabric, the actual decree-laws which are the subject of this afternoon's debate incorporate other measures of different nature, but which are very important in practice: speeding up the procedure for the public administration's hiring of all kinds of goods or services that are necessary - I do not have to explain anything else here today -; enabling the Ministry of Finance to make transfers between the various budget sections in order to strengthen the financing of health policy by the State; allowing the Government to control the direct or indirect taking of significant shares by third country companies in companies in strategic sectors in Spain; speeding up customs formalities for import and export in the industrial sector for six months; granting guarantees to facilitate the extension of the repayment periods of loans granted to agricultural holdings in order to cope with the drought of 2017, extending for one year the validity of the national identity document... In total, ladies and gentlemen, the Government of Spain today brings to its validation a set of real decree-laws that represent an important budgetary commitment, an important effort in terms of measures in these packages. We are talking about the mobilization of additional public spending, liquidity and guarantees of more than EUR 100 billion to inject into the economy and mobilize together with the financial sector up to EUR 200 billion, almost 20% of our gross domestic product. As you see, ladies and gentlemen, these are very complete, ambitious, powerful packages, which are complemented day by day by other decisions that the different ministries are taking within our competence to adapt to changing circumstances and as new needs are being identified, but we cannot forget that overcoming this situation does not depend solely on what the Spanish Government does, on what this Parliament does, on what the different public administrations do. We all need to put our part in the area of our responsibility to overcome this situation. Outside our borders, the European Central Bank has already made certain moves. The European institutions must also respond, which must give a fiscal response to a crisis which is symmetrical, which affects the whole of the Community bloc, which is not individual from any of the countries and which therefore requires a response which cannot be limited to the individual responses of the countries. We can only leave if we act together in health and in economic and social terms. It is time for Europe to demonstrate its strength by giving a common, strong fiscal response to the current circumstances. That is the position defended by the Spanish Government. We are also working on this, supporting ambitious solutions that will put into operation the instruments we have put in place to best deal with this exceptional situation also from a financial point of view. I do not want to conclude without mentioning two matters that we will also validate today with these royal decree-laws and which are matters prior to the triggering of the health crisis, but not least for that reason. Royal Decree-Law 6/2020 of 10 March includes the extension until May 2024 of the suspension of evictions from habitual housing of particularly vulnerable groups due to default of mortgage fees. This is a very important and necessary measure in view of the forthcoming expiry of the current moratorium, the extension of which gives tranquillity and security to more than 29 000 families. The Government has also decided to use the concept of vulnerable collectives to expand in order to protect debtors who, despite being in a situation of special vulnerability, were not beneficiaries until now of the ban on evictions. Thus, it is included among the assumptions of single-parent families, even if they have only one dependent child, and increases the maximum income limit of the family unit that serves as a reference to take account of the number of children and whether it is a single-parent family. In addition, the application of this moratorium is extended regardless of whether the housing contractor is a natural or legal person. The first of the Royal Decree-Laws for Validation also includes a provision supplementing the legal regime of the Bank Restructuring Asset Management Company, Sareb, adapting it to its special nature as an asset settlement entity to facilitate its continuity and the fulfilment of its mandate, including its social housing programme. To this end, its legal regime is amended so that it is not subject to dissolution because of a reduction in net worth to less than half of its share capital. Finally, the Royal Decree on the Development of the Law on the Management, Supervision and Solvency of Credit Institutions of 2014 is also amended to extend the type of already constituted financial institutions that can request their conversion into banks. With this extension, securities companies, payment institutions and electronic money institutions will also be able to apply, which means avoiding corporate restructuring processes, also improving the legal certainty of the Spanish financial system. Ladies and gentlemen, I am no longer extending. I have detailed a lot because these are three royal decrees-laws that have a lot of substance and that must be debated with the attention they deserve at this headquarters. I would like to conclude by stressing two ideas. Firstly, this crisis that we are experiencing is exceptional and also transitory. Our country is strong, it has important assets. The first, as it is proving - and throughout the afternoon in this Chamber we have been talking about it - is a mature and supportive population. We will get out of this situation without any doubt, but the decisions we make now and the responsibility with which we are able to act will depend to a large extent on the rapid and effective recovery after this difficult period. That is the philosophy that is guiding this Government and that will continue to guide us in the weeks ahead. They have no doubt that we will continue to act when it is necessary, where it is necessary and doing what is necessary to tackle the health crisis, to protect citizens and to ensure that the economy maintains a solid foundation on which to recover quickly. We will do so as before, following the recommendations of the health authorities, aligned with the countries of our environment, cooperating with the rest of the public administrations, which have the primary responsibility in the health and social field, listening to the social agents and without forgetting anyone, without leaving anyone unattended in these harsh circumstances, guaranteeing a safety net. Indeed, in the context of the extension of the state of alarm that the Government has requested of Congress, and which will be discussed below, we are already working on additional measures to support affected groups and businesses to continue to respond to the problems and situations that are emerging as decisions are taken at different levels. The second point I would like to share with you is that we need to continue to act together, with loyalty and responsibility. Citizens are following the authorities' recommendations on time, maintaining the maturity and temperance they have been showing so far, and thus helping to overcome the crisis. Public administrations must act in a coordinated way by supporting each other in the health, economic and social response. Financial institutions must provide liquidity and support to businesses, in particular SMEs and self-employed people, so that they can overcome this situation in the best possible way, and companies must provide their workers with the necessary security conditions and try to maintain employment by resorting to temporary employment regulation files only when they meet the legally provided conditions for public resources to be actually directed to those in need. This without forgetting the responsibility of the Government and Parliament, which is manifested today by the validation of these three royal decree-laws, with which we both fulfil our obligation to strengthen the health response, support the most vulnerable people and sustain the economic fabric and employment in order to try to avoid a more structural and lasting impact. That is why I would like to take this opportunity to thank all of you for your loyal collaboration, which I am convinced will continue to be the focus in the hard weeks ahead. I think that the Spanish citizens would not understand that it was otherwise when they themselves, with their behavior, staying in their homes, are collaborating in the fight against the disease. It depends on everyone tomorrow; that we row together depends on how and when we arrive at good port after the storm. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u68	Thank you very much, Madam Third Vice-President of the Government. Mr Martínez Oblanca was the first speaker on the joint parliamentary group's position.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u69	Thank you very much, Madam President. Good afternoon, Madam Vice President Third. The validation of these three royal decrees-laws must be only the first step for the Spanish to have the support with which to tackle the socio-economic difficulties resulting from the VOCID-19 pandemic. In line with our commitment, the Forum will support these three regulations, but we would like to stress that in order to increase their effectiveness they must be added to those which will complement the other administrations, which requires, ladies and gentlemen, the unlocking and corresponding liquidation once and for all of the 28 billion euros that the Spanish Government owes to the autonomous communities. Asturias, like the other communities, needs an additional plan for self-employed people and SMEs based on social commitment to equal opportunities and which addresses the great impacts that representative organizations are auguring around hospitality, travel agencies, textiles and clothing, trade, etc., on whom thousands and thousands of jobs depend and need protection in order to avoid their definitive destruction. I also consider it essential that the Government respond to the request to extend the legislative measures to support physiotherapists, podiatrists, occupational therapists, logopeds, optics and dietary, nutrition and other specialties for which the closure of their clinics, consultations and establishments has not been decreed, and will therefore have many obstacles to access to ERTs and public aid, as has already been warned by their general councils, colleges, organizations and professional associations. The reinforcement of all these urgent measures must also come, as I stressed at last Wednesday's plenary session, from supranational actions so that Spain can face the enormous socio-economic consequences resulting from the pandemic and guarantee the coverage of unemployment, the liquidity needs of companies and the generation of reactivating investment as long as private investment is reborn. I reiterate this once again: the national plans of each country have to incorporate a multiplier factor through the solidarity economic Europlan of the twenty-first century. The OECD itself, created at the end of the Second World War to coordinate the Marshall Plan and rebuild Europe, has already made a coordinated international effort to tackle the crisis resulting from the coronavirus on a large scale. To promote this colossal effort must be implemented without delay by the Government of Spain, and you, Madam Third Vice-President, have much to do there. Thank you very much.

Finally, as far as long-term measures are concerned, the Minister said - and I agree - that this is going to be a transitional situation. But the problem is that it's going to lengthen over time - all the indicators indicate that this isn't going to be a matter of a month - and it's not the same thing to stop the economy for a month than to have it stopped for a semester. This tells us that we are going to have to put an economic base almost again in many sectors that are going to push us forward so that long-term employment is broken down as little as possible and that economic sectors can be restarted. That makes us have to focus right now, because we have to be aware that our country's debt is alarming and yet we will have to continue to water the Spanish economy with liquidity; we will have to do so. Europe is fundamental. I believe that yesterday we all had bad news with the lack of agreement in the European forum. I don't think this is the right time. And, of course, instruments for national emergency characteristics, such as these, cannot accommodate a question of these characteristics. Thank you very much.

In this regard, with regard to health measures - the most urgent - and following the recommendations made by the World Health Organization, we call for the generalization of mass testing to be started by rapid testing of the majority of the population, starting with risk groups and people with mild symptoms. Equally urgent is the provision of personal protective equipment to all health-care providers in basic services, as well as the provision of equipment to those killed. We know that it is not easy, but it is necessary to protect those who protect us and to do so now. We therefore appreciate the Minister of Health's announcement on the purchase of masks, tests and breathing equipment, but we must also extend these protective measures and equipment to all those who work to provide essential services. Particular attention deserves, for its importance, the need for private health to be put at the service of the public system, ensuring the direct management of all the facilities, equipment and personnel with which private health is provided. With regard to social measures, we call for the suspension of mortgage payments and, as a matter of urgency, also the suspension of rents for persons in vulnerable situations, reduced income or affected by ERE, ERTE or redundancies; also for the suspension of the payment of essential service bills, such as electricity, water and gas, for vulnerable groups or with reduced income, and for paid leave for childcare for the duration of the emergency, which would be automatic for single-parent families and for one of the two persons in the case of two parents. With regard to economic measures, we propose a package of economic and social measures that guarantee livelihood income and public services for the entire population, because the key must be to guarantee public income and services so that the economic activity of companies can be resumed after the health crisis has passed. Thus, for the duration of the state of alarm, an emergency guaranteed income equal to the interprofessional minimum wage will have to be ensured so that any person who has substantially reduced his or her income can secure the vital minimum income that will enable him or her to cope with such loss of income. Measures should also be put in place for self-employed workers and small traders and companies with a special unemployment cover for precarious, fixed-term or temporary workers who are not currently entitled to cover but also accompanied by suspension of the liquidation of tax obligations and prohibition of lay-off in general, self-employed and any other form of non-payment of a monthly quota. With regard to the self-employed, we believe that the percentage of losses to benefit from the special benefit should be lower; it is now, in fact, 75%, as Minister Calviño has pointed out, but it should be lower and it should also be accompanied by the suspension of the payment of commercial rents and supplies. The ban on economic redundancies during the crisis is also a necessary step, both in the public and private sectors, as Italy has already decided. Measures should be taken to ensure employment by prohibiting redundancies, ERE and ERTE, in companies that exceed a certain billing threshold or have had profits in the previous year. As the European Union has already approved, the spending ceiling must also be left without immediate effect and the spending rule must be made more flexible for autonomous communities and municipalities. Similarly, since we cannot allow anyone to take advantage of this situation, we must avoid speculation on basic necessities, health care and cash, and to do so we must block the price of food products, basic necessities, as well as health care and transport. We are aware that all these measures represent a huge economic and financial effort for the public coffers, so we propose to take the measures that have not been taken for too long. We propose that not all the weight be left on the backs of the social majority, who is finally going to sustain all these measures. ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u77	Thank you very much, President. We jointly discussed the validation of the three decree-laws that preferably contain measures to stabilize the social and economic situation in order to cope with the impact of COVID-19. As described in the explanatory statement of the Royal Decree-Law 8/2020, the health crisis raised to an international pandemic by WHO has been transmitted to the economy and society, in part through the strengthening of measures to contain the pandemic, which have entailed restrictions on free movement and reductions in labour supply, resulting in a widespread breakdown of economic activity and an increase in market volatility, economic and social disturbances requiring urgent stabilization measures by the public authorities, in order to alleviate the fatal consequences in the form of loss of businesses, jobs and helplessness for the most disadvantaged who, if not adopted, would become structural. Ultimately, it is a matter of providing urgent support and protection to the productive and social fabric, as it is being done internationally, since the pattern of incidence of the health crisis in the productive and social fabric is common internationally. In this regard, we consider it justified to adopt the decree-law figure. It is a justified instrument from the beginning of necessity to deal with the serious situation that is going through and that requires rapid responses to the changing circumstances of the economic and social situation. The tsunami we are experiencing and its speed we perceive in view of the collapse of many of the references that soften our lives, from sport to the markets, the activity of services, economic activity, the advance of the pandemic, the burden of our health system, the cessation of education, 3434 deaths and 47,610 known infected, and this only in sixteen natural days in which three decree-laws have been passed, whose validation we are debating today, elections have been suspended, the WHO, as I said, declared an international pandemic, borders have been closed and the economy has been infected, resulting in its disruption, and we see an unprecedented social crisis close to the international level. That is why we consider, as I said, the extraordinary and urgent need to take action through decree-laws. Focusing now on the content of these three decree-laws whose validation we discussed, and without needing to go into detail in their concrete measures, which some will mention in the next item on the agenda, the Basque Parliamentary Group considers them steps in the right direction, although they are most likely to have to adapt according to the evolution of the situation generated by the coronavirus. As has already been pointed out, the effects this pandemic is going to have on the economy and on society are uncertain and indeterminate, but they are expected to be significant for the collapse, as I said, of markets and the paralysis of an important part of productive activity, which involves unemployment, which we hope will be temporary, for many workers. Therefore, a coordinated response from the entire institutional fabric, including the European one, is needed to mitigate the negative impact on the economy and people. Health measures, economic measures and social measures must interact; they must dialogue with each other in a balanced and dynamic way in order, containing the expansion of the pandemic, to try not to fall into an economic coma, in turn giving cover to people and groups in weaker situations. Now, more than at other times, it is worth remembering that it only goes faster but together it goes further, and without leaving anyone behind. In this regard, the measures contained in the decree-laws together with those implemented at all institutional levels, we believe that they are moving in the direction of strengthening the protection of workers; preventing people from seeing their purchasing power diminished; cushioning income losses due to lack of employment; maintaining the state of slowing down the productive fabric, without prejudice to compliance with security measures and combating this acute health crisis. These measures and initiatives will certainly require more, for which Europe's aid will be needed through mechanisms such as the MEDE, asset purchase programmes, suspension of tax rules and progress in the sharing of risks. And it is that the coordination of the response at European level is already an unavoidable necessity. In short, these decree-laws contain measures in the right direction, but for them to be effective they will require not only the speed in their implementation - some of them have a very limited duration of one month - but also not to lose sight of the indispensable formulation of an economic recovery plan for the future with measures, as I said, not only economic but also fiscal and social measures in view of the prospect that the crisis will extend over time. I shall conclude, Madam President, by saying that we will support the validation of the decrees-laws in considering, as I said, that they are steps in the right direction, without prejudice to the additional comments that I have already made to them through the motions for resolutions on the next item on the agenda and that, although many of them have not been accepted by the Bureau, I will detail them in the next item on the agenda. Thank you very much, President. Thank you very much, Minister.

With all this in mind, Citizens presented to the Government a series of measures aimed at facilitating that companies could extend social distancing, protect workers and alleviate the economic impact of the virus on employment; that companies and workers had at their disposal mechanisms to adapt working hours or working hours, set up or reassign staggered shifts to cover distance work; that companies could simply go to temporary employment regulation files to reduce working hours or suspend workers' contracts on a temporary basis, thus allowing employment to be maintained and avoiding the use of dismissals; that workers affected by an ERW were not required to be short-term unemployed or to face social security contributions. Today, self-employed people do not know whether or not they will be able to benefit from aid or under what conditions; there are professionals who are proud of this country and who do not know what their business is going to be, who look to the future with uncertainty. We cannot tell the self-employed to ask a bank for credit, to be indebted to pay the fee and taxes. This is another paradox of their shock plan; on the contrary, we have to allow them to paralyze the quota, social contributions and taxes until this crisis passes, and make them easier from the government; do not put them on more paperwork with the managers who are already up and down. Gentlemen of the Government, the self-employed always hold their shoulders, create wealth and employment and are one of the most vulnerable groups in this crisis. It's our duty to help you. For this reason, in Citizens we endorse the proposals of the associations of self-employed persons to freeze quotas and contributions, so that self-employed persons who have to take care of a minor or an older person for the closure of schools or centres of older persons do not pay quotas and can also benefit from these subsidies, so that Social Security takes charge of the quotas from the first day of leave and not from the two months as it happens now; that those who resume their activity after the coronavirus enjoy a flat rate that changes their quota of self-employed persons for one of 60 euros per month during the first year. We all have to get out of this crisis together. We can't leave anyone to their fate. We have a moral obligation to our autonomous. We're in time to rectify it and change it. Citizens are the party of the self-governing and we will continue to work to ensure that they have the support and help they deserve. Once we have guaranteed workers' wages and are betting heavily on our self-employed, we must focus on ensuring liquidity. It is very simple: that no solvent company sees its viability threatened by a timely credit problem - and here the 100 billion euros in guarantees come into play -; however, the actual decree does not detail the articulation of this measure. From here we ask the Government to put into practice, as soon as possible, the mechanisms for making the guarantees available to companies. And, abounding in our line of proposal, we ask that it be the financial institutions themselves that offer the credit lines, that the money reaches the company within two to five days, that the payment period is not less than two years, unless the customer requests it; that the interest rates be those corresponding to the most creditworthy credits; and, finally, we ask that ICO credits be able to cover the short-term liquidity needs of the companies, easing the risk and temporary conditions and requirements with which they are currently granted. From Citizens we have also urged a tax moratorium so that no company or self-employed person affected by this crisis has to pay tax on the income of individuals, VAT or corporate tax during this time. The measures contained in the second decree go in the right direction, Minister, although they remain short. We believe that the tax moratorium should have gone further, both from the point of view of taxpayers who can benefit and from the point of view of the amount of tax debts. In Citizens we advocate extending the tax moratorium to all companies, regardless of their size, and extending the moratorium also to social security quotas. Finally, we believe that the Ministry of Finance should postpone the filing and income tax of 2019, whose campaign starts in just two weeks. It is only fair that taxpayers, who have already been sufficiently damaged by this crisis, have the opportunity to postpone their income tax returns if they pay. Furthermore, we are concerned about the fiscal direction of this Government. We need a commitment that after the coronavirus crisis, taxes will not be imposed on families, workers, small and medium-sized enterprises or self-employed people. Citizens offered their support weeks ago for the urgent handling of national emergency budgets designed, designed and calculated to deal with this crisis. - Exactly the opposite of what has been raised so far. We need policies to support families and self-reliant people and to support businesses and not to destroy jobs. For this we need national emergency budgets that are serious, rigorous and that can square the accounts. Think about who you want to have next to you when the time comes to pay your debts. Our group reaches out to them at a time of responsibility for the good of all Spaniards. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a test of resistance from our citizens. As always, the Spaniards will be up to it and we will set an example. When we talk about ourselves, optimism is realism with trials, because there is nothing else to look back to know that we will go forward, as we have done, as we always do when we shake hands. We'll move on as we've always done when we've given our hand. Not so long ago, we overcame two world wars and were able to set up the European Union; we overcame a civil war and forty years of dictatorship; we were able to build an exemplary democracy that has brought the greatest period of peace, freedom and prosperity in our history. Ladies and gentlemen, we are a great country. Let us draw that conclusion from this crisis and try to remember it every time we return to this rostrum. Let us give the Spaniards the best tribute we can give them; let us be the echo of their union, let us bow our shoulders. Thank you very much.

An anecdote of an American feminist anthropologist, Margaret Mead, was told that one day a student asked her what was the first sign of civilization in a society, what was the first sign of civilization. The students made quinielas about whether it was the plow, a hook, a sharpening stone or the discovery of fire. The teacher responded by saying that the first sign of civilization that she could date in a society was a femur bone that had fractured and then healed, because it was impossible for a femur to have healed if it was not with the help of someone. In communities without civilization a femur that breaks down supposes that one cannot seek water, get food or flee from predators. The first healed femur indicated that, for the first time, in humanity we were not unknown to each other, but that we had institutions of solidarity that also cared for those who had had bad luck or the weakest. That's exactly the challenge we have ahead of us today. ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u89	Good afternoon, everyone and everyone. Thank you, Madam President. I would like to begin by joining the solidarity that has already been shown in that rostrum towards those affected and their families. I am also grateful, of course, for the work of health professionals, the State Security Forces and Corpses, the Armed Forces, as well as of all those who are in some way contributing their efforts to the solution of this situation that we are experiencing. And I also want to thank especially the workers of the Congress of Deputies for their effort, which today allows us to have this debate under consumable conditions, if not we could not be here. Turning already to the subject that affects us, I want to start by talking a little bit about the context in which we are in. Given the exponential worsening of the situation we have experienced in recent days, I believe that we all share a certain sense of lag by discussing the measures contained in this package of three royal decree-laws adopted a few days ago. We find ourselves in this acceleration of historical time that some social scientists have described regarding the convulsed periods: every hour is a Rubicon, every day is a definitive battle, every week is a century. Therefore, if anyone could qualify the measures that we validate today as excessive at the time of their approval, they hardly seem to be from Perogrullo analyzed from the perspective of here and now. That is why I note that the debate on these measures no longer focuses so much on their relevance as on their sufficiency. It is known that the Government has been taking a series of measures, embodied in these royal decree-laws on two major fronts, as the situation progressed. Already Minister Calviño has degenerate and detailed the same, but I just want to outline some of them. On the one hand, to address the first line of defence against the pandemic and thus strengthen the National Health System, for example, through the centralized and massive purchase of health and non-health supplies, also through the control of the prices of certain medicines or through the provision of additional resources for the autonomous communities. But there is also a second front, which is one that has been opened up to mitigate the economic and social effects of this crisis that is barely beginning to express itself with all its cruelty, especially in those traditionally most vulnerable sectors of our society. In this regard, I would like to focus mainly on the measures taken to deal with these sectors, which are the most concerned of my parliamentary group. Firstly, I should like to refer to the workers, as well as the self-employed. With regard to the former, the mechanisms associated with the temporary employment regulation dossiers are relaxed, the rules on access to unemployment insurance for these cases are relaxed and time suspended so that they do not compute for maximum periods. Conciliation measures are also established to care for families affected by the virus and teleworking is facilitated through the computer tools developed by Red.es, as well as the provision of an ICO credit line worth about 200 million to finance the non-personal digital adaptation of SMEs. In the case of self-employed persons, an extraordinary termination benefit is created which aims to cover the completion of the activity caused by an involuntary situation. On the other hand, this group will also be able to benefit from the 100 billion euro guarantee line announced by the Government in order to avoid a shortage of circulation, as well as the maturity of tax and financial obligations. The payment of certain taxes, equivalent to a three-month deficit, is also suspended in order to avoid treasury problems. As regards small and medium-sized enterprises, in addition to benefiting from the lines of guarantee and the tax suspension mentioned above, the exemption of 75% of business shares to social security is approved, which will become one hundred per cent for enterprises with fewer than fifty workers. The ICO's borrowing capacity is also expanded by some EUR 10 billion, as the minister has already announced, which will undoubtedly facilitate access to credit for these companies. The validated measures also place special emphasis on the tourism sector, one of the sectors most affected by this extraordinary situation. In this sense, the so-called Thomas Cook route to access finance is opened, which is extended by some EUR 200 million to the 200 already planned, as is the bonus for those fixed workers who are discontinuous in the sector. But there are many Spanish families who are overwhelmed by this situation and who require the determined help of the State for their survival. That is why I would like to highlight first of all the provision of an extraordinary social fund of 300 million euros, which will be channelled through the autonomous communities, for the support of social services, while at the same time releasing the surplus of local corporations for an equivalent amount to finance those social services that depend on them. A fund of 25 million is also set up to cater for the feeding of children from vulnerable families, a key element, since the closure of schools and their dining rooms has a decisive impact on the well-being of these families. Also, with the aim of meeting the acute needs of families, a moratorium is established on the payment of mortgage contributions, which is in addition to the measures already adopted in the first Royal Decree-Law 6/2020, which already extended the concept of vulnerability and extended its maturity to 2024. As I said at the outset, no one can doubt the relevance or urgency of these measures. If we are to ask ourselves, as other speakers have already done here today, what further, more radical, more incisive measures must be taken in the face of the relentless progress of this crisis. For example, in the area of housing, what measures should be implemented for the many Spanish families living on rent, or also for those who are currently unemployed and whose search for employment has been abruptly interrupted by this abnormal situation. Similarly, self-employed people continue to suffer from very specific problems despite the measures mentioned above for this segment and will surely need to be deepened into solutions for them. Today we have heard some proposals from some of the speakers. I am referring specifically to the spokesman for Citizens who has proposed several batteries for the autonomous segment, and I believe that some of them deserve this Government to study them. I am now also going to introduce a reflection on the political debate that we are holding that has to do with the structural conditions on the part of the State to deal with critical situations such as those we are experiencing today, an argument that has already been outlined in some way by my colleague Enrique Santiago in his previous speech and also by some other speakers. In this regard, I would like to denounce that the policies of cutbacks and austerity that have governed in recent years have substantially weakened our capacity for collective response, first and foremost in the area of health. It is worth recalling the fateful four consecutive years of reduction in health expenditure, between 2010 and 2014, four consecutive years of cuts, ladies and gentlemen, which have left us with 2180 beds less for health care than we had in 2009. So far only four autonomys have equaled or exceeded their historical peaks reached before the cuts, all the others - all the others - are below. But not only has our health care system suffered. According to Eurostat data, the burden of social protection spending on GDP, the safety net that defines a welfare state, has fallen with respect to 2009. In this sense, if we had 24.2% in 2009, we are currently at 23%. This shows that growth after the previous economic crisis has not been accompanied by an increase in social spending. In fact, if we exclude expenditure on pensions, we can see that the total expenditure on social protection is 15.4 % lower today than in 2009, which represents approximately 23.5 billion euros, which is said soon. Ladies and gentlemen, from the 1970s onwards, a new economic era, marked by deregulation and inspired by a perverse ideology called neoliberalism, began all over the world; an ideology that raised to the altars the mechanisms of the market and sought the systematic destabilization of State action. That era ends now, and it does not defeat it by the collective and conscious action of the billions of people who have been harmed by this dogma in recent decades. In a sort of irony parallel to that described by the British writer H.G. Wells in his famous novel The War of the Worlds the determining factor of the final outcome turns out to be microscopic. Even the former Minister of the Economy, Mr De Guindos, in an obvious crisis of faith, is now aiming at a minimum living income, which was as you know a flag of my political formation and which is currently being discussed in the Council of Ministers. One of the executors of the austerity plans passed now to convert. Who'd say! The most disciplinary economists calling for the suspension of the Leonine restrictions on the public accounts that they themselves helped to promote or promote directly. There would definitely be the following epitaph on the slab of neoliberalism: it only brought us suffering and helplessness. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

They are going to face serious liquidity problems that cannot be solved by a very stressed banking system, with limited capacity to help. Precisely, a contraction of the economy and its reducing impact on the income of companies can not only deprive them of the liquidity necessary to meet their daily operations, but will also trigger their lateness, with a negative effect on the already weakened financial position of the bank. If this dynamic is not prevented, the economic crisis will also result in a banking crisis and a deep recession. The economic policy strategy against an epidemic with systemic implications must be designed and programmed from the Government of the nation, becoming the role of autonomous governments a purely consultative role and ultimately a role in implementing the measures agreed upon by the central Government. Basically, the most urgent measures must be based on maintaining employment and facilitating the liquidity of the system; employment and liquidity, Madam Vice-President, these are the two keys. Dismissal must be the last resort and maintaining employment must be the priority of all. Therefore, in order to safeguard as many jobs as possible, we propose to add to your decree immediately and for at least three months after the end of the pandemic the 75% bonus of social contributions for businesses for the duration of the crisis, as well as the exemption of payment of the quota for all self-employed. Before we even look for liquidity injection mechanisms, we have to prevent that liquidity from coming out of SMEs, which account for more than 90% of our productive tissue. It would make no sense to remove the water from a plant to water it once it is dry, what must be avoided is the drying of the plant. We therefore propose to add to its measures the following: to relieve companies by extending the deadline for filing tax returns, which runs from March to April until 15 May; to postpone VAT collection and deductions from personal labour and leases from April to October in order to avoid cash outflows to self-employed persons and SMEs; to postpone the payment of the monthly VAT from March, April and May for large enterprises; to abolish the payment on 20 April of company tax and the payment on behalf of the IRPF for self-employed persons; to reduce the type of company to 12,5 % for this financial year 2020 and to extend the deadline for payment of this tax to 2019, at least until 25 September this year; to deduct from the share of the company tax on the costs incurred by companies to take measures in their establishments to avoid the extension of the pandemic, so as to recover the 100 % tax on such expenditure; to exempt families from VAT and from their special taxes on their private consumptions % of water, gas and electricity consumption, and to reduce all of the costs of electricity by reducing them by means of their own consumption. The urgency, priority and focus of our efforts right now is that the lack of liquidity does not destroy our productive fabric and that we get out of the crisis with as many companies as possible, that is, with as many jobs as possible. An effort to provide liquidity that the Government must extend to local entities. We therefore believe that the Government should agree with them on the release of the surplus generated in municipalities throughout Spain and the adoption of measures aimed at reducing municipal taxes for this year: IBI, garbage rates, public domain occupancy rates on terraces and municipal taxes for all traders. In that challenge to ensure liquidity, of course, we have to involve the bank with measures such as suspending the payment of the capital part in the mortgage repayment quota, while the state of alarm lasts and in the following three months, extending the maturity period for the time that such payment is suspended. To give an example: a mortgage loan of 1 % of EUR 150 000 remaining for twelve years is currently generating a monthly receipt of EUR 1105; during the period of this exceptional measure, the receipt would pass only the interest on the loan and would be only EUR 125 per month, that is to say, we would be releasing for that family an amount of EUR 980 per month for this extraordinary period. That is to inject liquidity based on preventing it from coming out of the reach of the families themselves. Similarly, we must allow the rescue of pension funds and savings plans without tax penalty, in order to alleviate the tensions of treasury in households, as they may be necessary to meet basic needs. That is the only political objective, at least the only legitimate political objective today. That is why we must work with perseverance, work according to the strategies that the experts are marking, with the maximum collaboration here, with communities, with municipalities, with our European partners, with the essential unity between the political leaders, with social discipline and with transparency. These are the recipes that work here and anywhere; there are no others, there are no better ones; there are no shortcuts; there are no magic formulas; there is no wise brother-in-law, a taumaturge who pulls us out of this in a flat fold. We must work with perseverance and listen to the experts. The three decree-laws that the Government brings to validation today respond to these sensible recipes precisely for three purposes: to strengthen the State in its fight against the virus, to protect workers, families, the most vulnerable in society at these difficult times and to ensure, as far as possible, a speedy and just reconstruction of our economy and our employment when this happens, which will happen. These are the objectives, they are the correct objectives, and the measures that include decree-laws are the same, so today we call for Congress' support for their validation. First, these decrees empower the Government to obtain the extraordinary resources with which to acquire health material, with which to derive precious resources from the health services of the autonomous communities and with which to promote scientific research for the vaccine and for the cure of the virus. These rules empower the Government to regulate the prices of health-care devices and to centralize purchases efficiently, which does not mean that the acquisition of equipment on its own has at any time been prohibited or restricted to any administration. It is clear that in a scenario of global demand and limited supply the Spanish Government has some extra possibility to access markets, to acquire with some advantage the products that our healthcare professionals need and to distribute them in an equitable and fair manner. That is what has been done; that is what is being done. Second, these decrees facilitate the availability of new resources and the release of own resources to finance social services, to better care for older persons and dependent persons, to ensure the feeding of children in vulnerable families. Discharge of electricity, gas, water is prohibited and a moratorium on the payment of mortgages to those affected by the coronavirus is approved. These are measures, ladies and gentlemen, which raise a genuine public shield against social insecurity; effective measures. There is also an extraordinary protection for workers by facilitating and speeding up the temporary employment regulation dossiers, the ERTEs. For what? For companies with difficulties to opt to temporarily suspend jobs before going to lay off. Thus, during the ERTE, companies will not pay social security contributions and workers will benefit from the unemployment benefit even if they have not contributed enough time. In turn, wage earners are allowed to reduce to 100 per cent of their time if necessary to meet the needs of reconciliation and care of their families at these difficult times. Self-employed workers affected by the crisis are also particularly protected. Tax payments are deferred to those who have to close. Those who see a drastic decrease in their billing do not have to pay social security contributions and also receive an extraordinary termination benefit. To protect self-employed workers; this is also the goal achieved by these Government decrees. Thirdly, measures to support economic activity, businesses, large and small are implemented in all sectors, with particular attention to those most affected, such as tourism. They are measures unparalleled in history by their nature and by their dimension. It is about small and large entrepreneurs being well prepared, well equipped to quickly rebuild the economy and employment as soon as we overcome the virus. A public guarantee line of up to EUR 100 billion is created, with additional guarantees of EUR 2 billion for SMEs and exporting companies, with extraordinary programmes to support R & D, digitisation and the implementation of work. We have been talking these days and we have been talking here today about a Marshall plan, a New Deal for Europe. Well, these resources that Spain is going to mobilize, together with the plans announced by the European Central Bank, together with the announcements made by the European Commission to stimulate demand, are probably the greatest mobilization of resources in the history of humanity; a gigantic effort that is being made with the aim of first defeating the virus and also overcoming the threat of recession, unemployment and poverty that is likely to follow. And it has to work, and it's gonna work. In short, the decrees clearly set the priority of the moment: to strengthen the State and the public services to double the virus. This is the priority among the priorities. There go the best efforts, all the necessary resources; whatever is needed, when it is needed and where it is needed, said the president, said our spokesman, and so it is being done. These decrees and other measures of the Government in the meantime protect the most vulnerable, those who need others to keep their families in decent living conditions. This time it will not happen as in the previous financial crisis, this time no one is left behind. There will be no winners and losers of the crisis. The virus is unfair to many people, because we cannot avoid it, but our response to the virus will not be unfair, because we can avoid it, and we will avoid it and we are avoiding it. With these decrees, the Government is also already working to ensure that the reconstruction of the destroyed economy, so that the recovery of lost jobs can take place as quickly, as effectively and as fairly as possible. That is why we insist so much that this will be a temporary crisis. We are not, as in 2008, faced with the paralysis of companies because companies are too indebted or not competitive or toxic bubbles have been inflated. No, companies now stop for the virus, and when the virus passes, those healthy and competitive companies have to regain activity and employment with the same vigour or with more vigour than before if it can be. This is the purpose of the historic, unusual effort being made by the Spanish Government and the institutions of the European Union. It's got to work and it's gonna work.
