ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u116	The next item is the request for authorization of the extension of the state of alarm, declared by Royal Decree 463/2020 of 14 March, for the management of the situation of health crisis caused by COVID-19. The President of the Government has the floor on behalf of the Government to explain the reasons for the request for extension.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u117	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, good night. I want my first words, like the first feelings of everyone, to be addressed to those who have suffered during these days the loss of a loved one, a father, a mother, a partner or someone to whom I have joined bonds of kinship, friendship or the bonds of love and affection. Similarly, I would like to express our solidarity with those who fight for their lives in hospitals or with those who remain sick in their homes. They and they are the top priority of all of us. For them and for them, our health care providers work tirelessly, for them and for them, as a matter of utmost urgency, we strive every minute of the day for public administrations at all levels of the State and in every corner of the country. For them, too, we all remain locked up. Ladies and gentlemen, eleven days have passed since the declaration of the state of alarm, eleven days in which we have been thrown into the mitigation phase, as the Minister of Health has repeatedly said, and in which we have pursued three key objectives: firstly, to put before the protection of those most exposed to the most severe forms of society; secondly, to shield the capacities of the health system to the maximum by protecting health professionals and those who cover basic services; and, thirdly, to protect the whole population by supporting basic hygiene measures with new measures of maximum social distance that avoid encounters between people and, consequently, reduce the possibilities of transmission. Ladies and gentlemen, there has not been a single day or an hour or a single moment when this government and all the public administrations - autonomous and local - have stopped acting. Since the Spanish Government, we have done so by deploying the measures taken at the last Councils of Ministers, both ordinary and extraordinary. We have already announced the mobilisation of up to EUR 200 billion to protect and assure families, workers, self-employed women and businesses; ultimately, our country. But we have also acted, ladies and gentlemen, first of all with health measures to strengthen public health and also support citizens through families, and here are the EUR 28 billion to the autonomous communities we are already transferring, the mobilisation of EUR 1 billion from the State's general budgets to the Contingency Fund, in this case managed by the Ministry of Health, as well as the set of actions to ensure the availability of medicines and also the necessary material. Intervention in prices of products necessary for health protection, creation of an extraordinary social fund with 300 million euros, extension of the protection of vulnerable groups in essential public services such as water, such as electricity, and also through the moratorium in the payment of mortgages. Secondly, measures for the maintenance of employment, in particular of small and medium-sized enterprises and of self-employed workers, granting the postponement and fractionation of their debts, as has been said here in the rostrum, encouraging teleworking in SMEs and promoting it through legal coverage, easing and speeding up ERTE with a view to prioritising the maintenance of employment on the termination of contracts, ultimately helping the self-employed concerned to have access to an ordinary unemployment benefit. Thirdly, measures to ensure the liquidity and flexibility of companies by opening the ICO's 400 million publicly guaranteed financing line, extending the bonus to discontinuous fixed contracts; and with a view to sustaining economic activity through liquidity guarantees, a guarantee line for companies and self-employed people of 100 billion euros is provided for yesterday, as you know, in the Council of Ministers for its effective granting. And finally, measures to support the necessary, essential and urgent scientific and medical research in the search for the vaccine against COVID-19. Ladies and gentlemen, we know that this pandemic has a threefold dimension: health, economic and also social, and therefore everything we do aims, first of all, to strengthen our health system and advance us to possible health threats, guaranteeing health security; mitigating the economic consequences of COVID-19 and recovering ourselves to rebuild the country in the shortest possible time in the economic and social sphere, and finally to protect all Spanish citizens, guaranteeing their right to life, and more precisely a life in decent conditions. I would also like to inform you of the various orders, resolutions and instructions issued by the four ministers designated as competent delegated authorities since the last time I appeared at the Congress of Deputies on 18 March. The Minister of Health has adopted two interpretative criteria of the Royal Decree declaring the state of alarm in order to clarify that public transport is permitted to: first, persons with behavioural disorders, such as persons with diagnosis of the autistic spectrum and disruptive behaviors whose situation can be aggravated as a result of confinement, provided that they are carried out with a companion and that they respect the measures necessary to prevent contagion, and persons who carry out voluntary activities for the feeding, rescue and veterinary care of domestic animals living in urban public spaces. The Minister of Health has also issued eleven orders in his field of responsibility. The first is to suspend the opening of hotels, tourist accommodation and short stay accommodation located in any part of the national territory and regulate the phased closing process. However, as the Minister of Health has also stated, this does not prevent these spaces from being able to be used for health purposes, either in consultation or in hospital. For his part, the Minister of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda has also issued an order to guarantee accommodation and food in hotels to those workers who have to perform tasks of supply and general interest, health care, as well as to persons who have to travel to care for the elderly, minors, dependants, persons with disabilities, especially vulnerable persons or persons with health care needs. The second order of the Minister of Health is to ensure the maintenance of electricity supply to producers of metals, chemicals and other products of quantitative relevance in the markets. The third, to recommend to the Minister of Justice the coordination of the professional activity of officials of the Administration of Justice Service in order to ensure the continuity of essential services and, at the same time, to safeguard the health of service users and public employees. The fourth order is intended to ensure access to pharmaceutical benefits for groups belonging to special social security schemes. The fifth sets out measures for the management of household waste, as well as the management of waste from hospitals, ambulances, health centres, laboratories and similar establishments in contact with COVID-19. The sixth seeks to give final destination to the deceased with the greatest agility possible, eliminating the need for twenty-four hours from the death to the granting of the burial license. The seventh ensures the proper functioning of the supply services to the population of water for human consumption, as well as to the sanitation of urban waste water, by giving consideration to essential services to test laboratories, manufacturers and dealers of reagents, as well as to supplies of goods and spare parts of support services. The eighth sets out obligations for the provision of information, supply and manufacture of certain medicinal products which are considered essential for the management of the crisis in which we are immersed. And two orders have also been issued to regulate the activities of seniors' homes - which I know is a shared concern in this House - in order to reduce the risk of contagion and treat people suffering from this disease in the most appropriate way. One of these includes, first, protective and clean-up measures; and, second, the obligation to isolate residents diagnosed with COVID-19 and respiratory infections. Finally, a third, which are the coordination measures for the diagnosis and monitoring of residents. The other, adopted yesterday, establishes for the residences the obligation to maintain their activity, unless the competent authority determines and regulates the intervention measures of the private residential centers that I announced on Sunday. For his part, in the last week, the Minister of the Interior has issued two orders. The first of these is intended to ensure the provision of essential products and services to the population, and for this purpose temporarily suspends the restrictions on movement for goods and the special traffic control and surveillance campaigns that were planned. It shall also extend until 60 days the driving licences and licences which expire during the state of alarm. The second order lays down the criteria applicable to refuse entry into our country of persons who are not nationals of the European Union or of countries included in the Schengen area, while agreeing also to the temporary closure of the posts authorized for the entry and exit from Spain through Ceuta and Melilla. The Minister of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda has also issued five additional orders, in addition to the one mentioned above, and an instruction, in which, in the first place, it enables the same Ministry to acquire centrally a maximum of 8 million FFP2 masks, with a view to their subsequent distribution in the field of transport to persons providing services in areas of risk. Secondly, the validity of certain administrative titles issued by the Directorate-General of the Merchant Navy is extended in order to ensure the continuity of the activity and, at the same time, to suspend the planned inspection and recognition activities by the maritime authority and the maritime administration. Thirdly, instructions are issued on the carriage of goods by road allowing two persons to go to the cab of the vehicle when necessary because of the type of transport to be carried out, and also authorizing the opening of repair and maintenance workshops, as well as of sales establishments for parts and accessories with direct sale to the workshops, although they must remain closed to the public. Fourthly, drivers' qualification cards accrediting the professional qualification certificate are allowed to be used which cannot be renewed as a result of the suspension of continuing training courses or the impact of extraordinary measures on the regular functioning of the competent administrative bodies. Fifthly, measures for the transport of animals in order to ensure the logical supply. Sixthly, the conditions are laid down for the provision of air transport services on the routes, which are essential in a reality that is the island of Palma de Mallorca, Menorca and Ibiza and this service is awarded during the state of alarm. Ladies and gentlemen, Spain has always followed, as has been said here by other speakers, the recommendations of the international health experts, of the World Health Organization itself and also of the extraordinary team of professionals of the Centre for the Coordination of Alerts and Health Emergencies, whom we will always thank for their dedication, their commitment, and of course also for the European bodies. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, as you see, these are health measures, social measures, economic measures and labour measures and I repeat - I repeat - that the Government, and in particular the four ministries with delegated powers and myself as President, has not left a moment to take action, to issue orders and I assure you that they will continue to take action to meet the various challenges that may arise in the coming days. I would like to make a special mention of the actions and deployment made as never before in the democratic history of our country by the State Security Forces and the Armed Forces, a total of 240,245 troops deployed between the National Police, the Civil Guard, the Ertzaintza, the Mossos d'Esquadra, the Foral Police, the Canarian Police and the local police. And in the case of the Armed Forces, a total of 2,680 troops in more than 135 cities, supporting different social services, the installation of temporary infrastructure and the carrying out of tasks of recognition of needs, distribution of food and disinfection of stations, airports, courts, hospitals and residences of older people. They are also transferring patients between hospitals, to hotels, as well as the manufacture of medicines and sanitary devices. The mobilization of all public representatives, ladies and gentlemen, is maximum, it is total. All of them and all of them work together to win the war on the virus with our more than 350,000 professionals from the National Health System. They are as a whole, ladies and gentlemen, a national pride. Ladies and gentlemen, the magnitude of the challenge facing humanity is becoming increasingly evident. The virus advances and strikes hard at every country in the world, ignoring the distances, level of development and political orientation of its Governments. Health systems in countries that we all think are excellent are overwhelmed by the strength of the number. That, the overwhelming number of infected, is the power of the virus, that's its strategy. One by one, the containment tactics that each country has been testing, are being overcome until, finally, the only option is the social isolation and confinement of the bulk of the population in their homes. Precisely because isolation is indispensable to deal with the virus, today I come before you in this House to ask for an extension of that confinement, because in a society of free men and women, such as the Spanish one, the decision to lock us up in our homes cannot be decreed or imposed, but approved by the citizens themselves through their legitimate representatives. That is what establishes our democracy and our Constitution. I know, ladies and gentlemen, that it is not easy to prolong the state of alarm, it is not, and with it the containment measures, which are not. It is not easy for the Government to request it, it is not easy for its lords to grant it, but it is even more difficult for citizens to assume it. I am convinced that the only effective option against the virus is social isolation. All of us and all of us, regardless of our political colour, are convinced of that. And I am also sure that everyone, regardless of our ideology, is aware that this rule must have some exceptions, that we need in maximum security to be able to leave our homes to make the purchase or to buy medicines and that it happens that for supermarkets to have food and pharmacies medicines are necessary some other economic activities of production and transport. And it happens that while we are in prison, we must preserve the maximum of the goods that will allow us to resume our lives once this nightmare is over, which will soon be over and over, but, of course, we have to address that reconstruction task. So, when it comes to action, it is necessary to use the scalpel in order not to amputate any nerve in our social body that compromises the very support of the confined population or the subsequent recovery of that same population and that forces us, ladies and gentlemen, to seek a very difficult balance, a delicate balance, but we must do so. We must win the virus without leaving anyone behind; it has already been said here and we share it. That is the balance that is sought with the decree that the Government brings today and in which we must find each and every political force present here. We have locked ourselves up to preserve ourselves from the contagion and thus preserve everyone from the contagion. We have locked ourselves up to buy time, ladies and gentlemen, so that hospitals can assimilate the number of patients that come to them, time for researchers to find effective medicines to treat the disease, until they finally get a vaccine that protects us all. It's a new sacrifice for everyone and it's an extreme sacrifice for people who were already living very delicate circumstances before this whole nightmare happened. Ladies and gentlemen, for many and for many, confinement rather than a precautionary measure can, unfortunately, imply the intensification of a previous nightmare: women mistreated by their partners, with whom they are forced to live; people living in infra-habitats, lacking ventilation and a minimum living space; people with psychological problems, who now see their connection with the world broken, allowing them to have minimal stability. Ladies and gentlemen, we also know the sacrifice that the extension of the state of alarm entails for all of them and all of them, and we ask that they, too, protect their health and can aspire to a better tomorrow than they had been enduring yesterday. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear before the House to request an extension, as I said earlier, to request time. It is time that allows the adaptation and resistance of our health system, and it is a matter of time that science finds effective treatments and develops a vaccine that eliminates the virus. In the meantime, the vaccine is each of us, because we not only have to flatten the curve in the shortest possible time, and that is where the government is, but we try to avoid future waves. That is very important, ladies and gentlemen, we must try to avoid future waves and anticipate future threats. During this situation the Government is complying, as it cannot otherwise be, with the provisions of Organic Law 4/1981 of 1 June, and with Article 116 of the Spanish Constitution, which regulates the state of alarm, so that the social and democratic state of law that we have, the guarantees of freedoms and rights that the Constitution recognizes and protects even in such an exceptional situation - this Government is not insensitive to this - is always guaranteed. Ladies and gentlemen, I am sure that the Congress of Deputies and the political forces that make up it will understand the national emergency situation in the country and I hope that they will support the extension of the state of alarm until 11 April, because it is essential to defeat the virus to continue to deprive it of its main weapon of diffusion, which are our own social contacts. I compare, therefore, ladies and gentlemen, to the House to ask for time and I also appear before the House to propose another equally precious good at a time as critical as the present one, which is unity and loyalty. At the first appearance I made to present the royal decree of the state of alarm I said that when this crisis ended the Government was going to propose to the House the creation of an evaluation commission on what has happened to extract the lessons that we have to draw as a society and as a state so that in the face of future pandemics that will inevitably come we will have the necessary mechanisms to make our society as resilient as possible to them. Ladies and gentlemen, I would also like to move today that, during the period of extension of the state of alarm, I propose to the House that the Parliamentary Committee on Health should become the committee to monitor the crisis in COVID-19 and that, therefore, all the information and data provided by the Centre for the Coordination of Alerts and Health Emergencies and that, of course, it should be known every day by the Technical Committee to monitor the state of alarm, which is composed of the four authorities and by myself, can be shared daily with the Parliamentary Committee and with all the political forces involved there. I also move the commitment, ladies and gentlemen, that the Minister of Health will appear once a week in this Commission before all the parliamentary forces in order to be able to explain the evolution of the pandemic and the measures we are taking. Therefore, maximum transparency, maximum collaboration, maximum coordination and loyalty and unity of the Spanish Government with the Congress of Deputies. I must also say to you, ladies and gentlemen, that there are many very reasonable elements in the amendments that have been tabled by each and every political force. And I would like to tell you that the Government of Spain, regardless of the outcome of those votes, is studying each and every one of the measures that you have put forward in your amendments so that those that are feasible can of course make them credible during this period of extension of the state of alarm. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, I come to ask you for time, as I have said before, and to ask you for something also very precise at this very critical moment, which is loyalty and unity, because, ladies and gentlemen, every need we meet together, every problem for which we find a solution in cooperation is another step towards the recovery of the life of Spanish citizens and the reconstruction of the common well-being. Let us continue to work with the utmost institutional loyalty, ladies and gentlemen, to preserve unity as a response to the sacrifice we are asking the citizens who remain in their homes, we will persevere in that loyalty as a response to the courage of those who leave their homes to fight the virus. Today all Spaniards and Spaniards must be united, and also with a specific territory, with the Community of Madrid, as tomorrow we will be, ladies and gentlemen, with Catalonia and with each and every one of the autonomous communities that need it. Everyone, from the most remote municipality to the Government of Spain, from every citizen in his confinement to every health care provider in his robe, suffered the same anguish in the face of a phenomenon that we had never experienced and had not even imagined. We all feel a similar sadness, but that is precisely why we must act with maximum loyalty and with maximum unity. I wish politics had the extraordinary power to restore normality to our lives immediately and in a matter of seconds - I wish we had that power - but it does not, ladies and gentlemen; what does make it possible for us is the power of cooperation, it is the power of coordination, it is ultimately union. That is why I reiterate in this House the same invitation that I transferred to the presidents and autonomous presidents in the two conferences that I have held with them in the last ten days, and that is that we work together, let us think about the anguish with which the spread of the epidemic continues, the major and the most vulnerable groups in the face of this pandemic, let us think about the health workers and also about their awakenings; let us flee, ladies and gentlemen, any petty thought, any selfish thought. No political leader is endowed with superhuman powers, but we are very lucky to have the response of a formidable citizenry and the scientific advice of the best professionals. We just need to add to those two factors our spirit of union and our will to victory, which we have. Let us act, ladies and gentlemen, with the time given to us by the citizens and with unity, with the time we need and with loyalty. I count on it, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you, President. ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u140	Ladies and gentlemen, no one in his or her right mind will deny that the measures contained in this alarm decree are necessary to control the spread of that virus which every day brings society to a permanent state of uneasiness, concern and fear. And no, we don't propose to give the switch and suddenly turn off the activity. Because the reality is that what is achieved through house confinement is lost by the obligation to attend non-essential face-to-face work. That is why we say that the suspension of all this non-essential economic activity must be considered. We were already late yesterday. In all scenarios, a time is coming when much of industrial production is being closed. It happened in Wuhan, it has passed in Italy - late, but they have done so - and yesterday Quebec, with 628 confirmed cases of coronavirus and 4 deaths, has paralyzed its non-essential economic activity. Finally, we are dealing with the debate between production and health, and we believe that health is the priority in this debate. The economic consequences of the measures we are proposing must also be assessed, but not only those affecting large companies, which is what is being thought of when the measure of paralysing non-essential economic activity is rejected. For the popular classes fear of the virus is added the panic of losing work, the lack of resources to deal with bills or the risk of losing basic rights such as housing, sufficient food or the well-being of minors. The objective of the institutions should be to design an orderly closure of non-essential economic activity and a plan to address its economic consequences. The families, the workers, the workers, the industrial and commercial fabric on the street are the ones who need to be rescued now. We are facing a drama for public health, but this is also a humanitarian drama, a drama that clearly shows that in the neoliberal economic model the dominant logic establishes that we live as we can and leave ourselves, that we live to work and do not work to live decent lives. First, public health was weakened by cuts and today we are condemned to work so that the wheel of capitalism does not stop, even if the fuel that moves that wheel is our own lives. Our lives, ladies and gentlemen, people's lives are worth more than the economic benefits of big companies and capital. On the other hand, I would also like to point out that, from the point of view of Euskal Herria, this decree has intervened from a plume of essential competences for the Basque Autonomous Community and Navarra and it has again been shown that forty-two years have not been enough to comply with the statutes but that in a minute they are able to abolish them. In any case, and leaving this debate aside today, we do want to make it clear that it is also a huge mistake to believe that such centralisation ensures the automatic effectiveness of the decisions taken. The competent authorities of the Basque Autonomous Community and Navarra and the local institutions have an irreplaceable knowledge of the specific problems, a knowledge which is for the general benefit of a policy of collaboration, even more so in a situation like that which we live in. Therefore, in our view it is a wrong strategy. It is not the same Madrid as Zalduondo or Barakaldo, it is not the same Madrid as Barañain or Altsasu. The management of the crisis requires a detailed analysis of the contagion process and it is essential to do the opposite of what the Government has proposed: we must give resources and decision-making capacity to administrations such as Basque or Navarra so that they can develop an effective strategy according to the specific situation of each place, because it is our institutions that are on the ground. Finally, I cannot resist commenting on the Spanish Army's omnipresent presence on television and media. This is not about Spanish patriotic sophasia or understanding a humanitarian crisis in terms of war. Social protection and health resources, that's all. It's worth whitening the image of the Spanish Army. Enough press conferences with military lexicon, patriotic speeches, medals and galons. You can't start a press appearance on a day when there are hundreds of dead with the phrase: No news on the front. You can't. Less military and more sanitary, because this is not a war, ladies and gentlemen, this is a catastrophe, and they are not soldiers who respond to us with all their courage to this crisis, they are doctors, nurses, cleaners, cashiers, taxi drivers, farmers, and these heroes do not wear torches or medals, wear white robes, wear thermometers, wear gloves and carry a humanity worthy of admiration. Because no, you don't have to prepare for a war, but for life, and life can only be defended with public spending on health, education and research, and public money must be to investigate systems, to stop epidemics, to make diagnoses and vaccines, as well as to protect the most vulnerable groups. We know that the state of alarm must be broadened, of course, in the same way that we know that in 15 days' time we will be here again to give the green light to a further extension, because it will be necessary, and we will say again that it is insufficient if the necessary measures are not taken. That is why our vote will be on abstention, because we do not believe that everything we think needs to be done to stop this pandemic. In any case, we are convinced that we will emerge from this crisis. And when someone has the temptation to win from some power, we will remind you that this was possible because of the work, delivery and solidarity of the popular classes, nurses and nurses, doctors and doctors, cellists, research technicians, supermarket workers, pharmacy employees, and all the workers who maintained the activity necessary for life to continue. He approved it when we had 4231 infected; Italy approved it with 9172 infected, the United Kingdom with 5687 infected and France with 6573 infected. Spain passed the state of alarm decree when, unfortunately, in our country - and there are many - we had 120 dead, the United Kingdom 281 and Italy more than 400 Italians. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, we must be humble and place the issue at its true point, because all the countries have been invaded surprisingly. About March 8th. Ladies and gentlemen, almost all European countries celebrated the demonstrations on 8 March with absolute normality. This was the case in Paris, Berlin, Vienna or Brussels. In France there were even demonstrations of the yellow vests until Saturday 14 March, when we declared the state of alarm, and even the next day the first round of the municipal elections in France was held and, logically, they had to suspend the second round of those elections. This is the first consideration I wanted to make to the various groups that have criticized this alleged lack of endurance or late decision on the presentation of the state of alarm. Mr. Casado, you lead a party that rules in different autonomous communities, some of them very affected by the coronavirus pandemic, and therefore you should know how to distinguish the urgency from the importance at a time when the sense of state is essential. Now the urgent thing - I repeat, the urgent thing - is to slow down the propagation curve, to resist the toughest days - I have said it in the various appearances, that this week was going to be very hard, very hard - and to prepare ourselves for possible repercussions of the pandemic while we are looking for a vaccine. We live, ladies and gentlemen, exceptional times that require exceptional attitudes and sacrifices. When, for example, farmers stop their legitimate protests to focus on filling our storehouses or when Germany - and here it has been remembered by some intervener - is willing to break its fiscal orthodoxy and assume a historic public debt we are sent a clear message, and it is time to be united. Look, Mr. Married, in this battle you and the opposition as a whole are all allies to me in front of the virus. Now we can't afford differences. What I am demanding is unity and loyalty, which is what I said in my first intervention, the same, by the way, which shows the opposition forces where other political parties govern, such as you, for example; the loyalty of the opposition on this issue in Madrid, Andalusia, Castile and León or Murcia, or, if you prefer, the loyalty shown in most of the countries in our European environment by the opposition forces; the loyalty, for example, of the British Labour Party; the loyalty of the Portuguese right, which expresses by the mouth of its spokesman - and I quote textually - the following words: We do not see them as the government of an opposing party, but as the Government of Portugal to which we must all help at this time; the loyalty of the opposition of the right-wing in France, when they manifest - and quote textually -: In view of the extent of the epidemic, we do not speak of a majority and of an opposition, what there is a single united and united National Assembly. Ladies and gentlemen, Mr Casado, the extent of this tragedy was not known, nor was it known at the time when it was to begin, nor was it known by the political leaders of the other countries; nor did you know it, nor do we know what day and how much damage this epidemic will bring to an end. If anyone knew, as I said a week ago, I would go up to this rostrum and announce it, but no one will, ladies and gentlemen. What we do know is that this will end sooner and with less damage if we think together, if we act together and if we fight together. That is what I formally propose to you today: unity and loyalty. I also ask for solidarity on a day like today with the House as a whole. Solidarity especially with the Community of Madrid, whose figures are tragic and overwhelm us all, in particular the neighbours and neighbours of the Community of Madrid. Such solidarity is now more necessary than ever. Madrid has always been, ladies and gentlemen, very generous with all of Spain. Madrid has always welcomed everyone, no one feels outside Madrid, and now Madrid needs the whole of Spain, ladies and gentlemen. Today is Madrid, but tomorrow, as I said in my initial speech, it can be Catalonia, and the day after tomorrow other autonomous communities, and we must be united to win the virus. The virus, as you know, is attacking the Community of Madrid with greater virulence in this first phase. The situation of the murdered and the hospitals in our capital is on the verge of collapse, and this is the moment when all Spaniards will be in solidarity with the Madridns. The Government of Spain shall ensure equity and provide health resources to hospitals in the Community of Madrid coming from other parts of Spain which do not logically have these saturation problems. That solidarity of all Spaniards will help Madrid at this critical moment and later that solidarity will be with Catalonia and the other territories of our country that are in need of it. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, I ask the House as a whole - I insist - for time, unity and loyalty. It is what we need on the front to win this war against the virus: time, unity and loyalty; the same time, the same unity and the same loyalty that I offer you. Ladies and gentlemen, I assure you that when we get out of this, you will have plenty of time to oppose the government, a lot of time, but I am already telling you that there will be a difference with today, and that is when I will defend myself. Now I am not going to lose a minute or a gram of energy in defending myself, because I need all the time and all the forces the Spanish Government has at its disposal to fight the virus. The enemy is the virus and I hope we all understand it. [[Applauds]] Since when did the various authorities or you, Mr. Married, know that you had to collect masks or breathers and robes? From 14 March, i.e. from the moment the Government of Spain declares the state of alarm. That day many saw clearly what they had not seen before. This is the state of alarm, which is an exceptional state, extraordinary, out of the ordinary. It is not that it limits rights to the territories, ladies and gentlemen, it is that it limits rights to citizens, who, among other things, cannot leave their homes freely, and the rights of citizens are the most inalienable, so that we are very measured when assessing the restriction of competences. I also told him at the appearance in which I explained the state of alarm to the whole House. You know this is going to last as long as the fight against the virus lasts, not another day. This does not undermine diversity, plurality or statutory rights. It's a state of alarm, so let's not forget it for a second. I do not think that citizens are very concerned that the disinfection of Bilbao airport, the port or the airport of Barcelona is done by the army; they are concerned that it will be disinfected. This is the question, ladies and gentlemen, and hence also the protection of the state of alarm. With regard to the spokesman for the Basque Nationalist Party, I believe that the proposal we have put forward to turn the Parliamentary Health Commission into the Pandemic Monitoring Committee where we provide the various parliamentary groups with the information you need - not only daily information on the evolution of the pandemic, but also on the measures being taken by the Government of Spain - seems to me to be taking up the glove of the amendment proposal you put forward in your resolution. There have been some speakers who talk about the confinement and that we have to be tougher and stricter with the confinement: Mr Rufián, Mr Errejón, Mr Baldoví and Mrs Aizpurua. I have always said that we have to remain very flexible and attentive, and I really appreciate a constructive willingness to approach this issue, but I can assure you that the health authorities who manage this crisis study with the utmost attention every report, suggestion or proposal that comes to us from highly qualified professionals and researchers. As you know, there are reports that have different predictions. The long-term forecasts that are set are risky in some cases; they may be true, I am not saying no, but they may also lead to major miscalculations. As I have repeated on several occasions, and this is really the substantive thing in this place where we are, the Government and its chain of command, as the spokesman of the Socialist Parliamentary Group has said, are at all times obeying the instructions and protocols of the World Health Organization, which has acknowledged this, praising, of course, the action of the Government of Spain. I think I can say that we are an exemplary country in the implementation of the recommendations. I could give you figures on the evolution of mobility in our country, which is precisely how we are moving the stoppage and economic slowdown that we have right now in our country. The energy consumption of households or services is also eloquent enough to be aware of the period and the economic consequences that it is going to have and that it is already having in terms of the destruction of employment, but also of the disappearance of enterprises; it is eloquent enough to be aware that we have to be balanced and balanced in terms of the restrictions that we impose on the whole country. There is no absolute certainty in this matter, ladies and gentlemen, there is no such certainty, but there are no contradictions either. This is what I think is important as regards the definition of government action. There is a very broad consensus on the direction in which we must walk. We all agree with the basic lines of avoiding unnecessary mobility and reducing economic activity that is not essential. Well, what's essential, what's not essential? Therefore, we do not discuss contrary proposals, but the limits and scope of the same measure. As I said in my speech, we must carefully calculate the limits of the confinement and paralysis of our economic activity. I recall that when I presented the royal decree of the state of alarm here I suggested that all the actions that the Government of Spain had taken were based on three fundamental criteria emanating from the guidelines of the World Health Organization. The first has to do with solitary confinement measures, the second has to do with socio-economic impact measures that have such containment measures and the third has to do with respect for human rights. And in that difficult balance between those three guides that the World Health Organization is marking, we are working from the Government of Spain. As the WHO warns, this, confinement, could in turn have serious short-term consequences, including health, and devastating effects on the indispensable reconstruction phase that we should soon face. Therefore, let us not make this a cause of faith or an ideological debate, as some speakers have raised from this rostrum. Let's not fall into that nonsense, that's all I'm asking. Let us not force citizens to take sides in a debate that is technical and scientific and in which the public authorities make decisions based on the advice of experts. That is what the Government of Spain is doing. As for the comments on the Army and the Armed Forces and some appearances that have been raised by some speakers, I say again what I said a moment ago, that it is about mobilizing another public sector, in this case one more, that has the training, that has the capacity and that it has the means to help certain groups in certain situations of this emergency that we are experiencing. Therefore, it is a matter of summarizing. The recourse to the Armed Forces is not done at the expense of anything or anyone, they are not left behind, they simply add up. They're one more instrument. Why can't we count on him? The Armed Forces do not replace doctors, nor nurses; they do not replace other state or autonomous security forces, but they reinforce and complement them, ladies and gentlemen. They are limited to helping where they can do so, to free other public servants from certain tasks, so that they can meet other different needs. Is that bad? I don't think so. The care and protection of strategic infrastructures is being carried out today by the Army because this allows for the release, for example, of the National Police, other State Security Forces and Bodies, to do another type of service or work that is undoubtedly important at this time. Is it bad that they empower field hospitals where they may be needed and that they explain it in the media and in the press? I don't think so. And take care of giving shelter and protection to homeless people? I don't think so, ladies and gentlemen. And that they explain it in the media so that it is also known with absolute transparency information what they are doing? Finally, we all have our subsidiaries and our phobias more or less rational, but the least that can be expected of us, ladies and gentlemen, in a crisis like the present is that we keep them out of the way and do not interfere. It is true that when we say that all essential services must be stopped and decided by the Government, we could go into the casuistics of which are and which are not, but I do not think it is technical. There is no more political decision than deciding who is at risk of living and who is not; it is concentrated politics. I am sorry and I know that it will be unpopular and I do not believe in this sort of self-help manual thing and that at the moment we have to leave ideas and leave politics; it is on the contrary, it is right now when we have to decide on one course or another, and we propose one: we have to paralyse the country completely and all activities that are not essential and here we are saying that the government will decide it. I give you two examples: the Burger King and the works. Are the technical criteria that determine that they are open? Well, I guess there's a lot of interest going on here. I ask the Government of Spain, which is a progressive government, to go at least as far as the so-called non-progressive governments of France and Italy are going. Secondly, what we say about health: we must put all resources at the service of public health, we must convert the whole industry to the sole objective of serving the national confrontation against the virus and with what has to do with the national reconstruction programme, we must ensure that confinement is not afraid, we must ensure that confinement is not poverty, that it is not excluded, firstly, so that those who stay at home stay more calm and stay calm and, secondly, because reconstruction is not going to start on the day we leave our homes, it starts now, it starts by determining how many jobs are lost or how many businesses are lost and it starts now preventing us from finding a desolate landscape when we leave. I do think it is time for politics, so I propose a course that first has to go to the people, that first has to stop Spain and take care of the confinement and that for no one the confinement is poverty, is fear or is social exclusion. I know that there are other interests at stake - politics is always about deciding interests - and at the moment I think you would have immense support in Spanish society if you put the interests of health and tranquility of all Spaniards ahead of the possibility of making profit from, say, private groups of privileged interests. It is true that they are not antagonistic positions, it is true that they are not antagonistic discrepancies, but in this case I fear that time and the difference in taking some measures now or taking them in two weeks may have much to do with pain and suffering, especially for working families, who are carrying this with the most difficulty. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u167	Thank you very much, Mr Errejón. For the Citizens' Parliamentary Group, Mrs Muñoz Vidal has the floor.

I should like once again to emphasize the importance of unity, the importance it will have for our democratic history, that today, from the opposition, there are responsible parties that support this Government at a critical and decisive moment for our country. You know, President, this government knows that from Citizens we will act with maximum loyalty and responsibility, but also with total transparency and with the firm conviction that this state of alarm, this sacrifice of millions of Spaniards really serves to defeat the virus. Ladies and gentlemen, I insist that we be judged today on how we act and we will have to learn from all of this. I am proud to be part of a project for the Spaniards, who are reaching out to present proposals to improve the lives of all without thinking about ideological clichés or party interests. From this crisis, ladies and gentlemen, we will come out more united, I have no doubt, but also with a clear idea of who has come to this House today to support the Government of your nation and who has come with identity and ideological demands. In Citizens we will always be with the interests of our country, trying to give back to the Spaniards everything they are giving us in this crisis: unity, solidarity and responsibility. ParlaMint-ES_2020-03-25-CD200325.u172	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, we have again held an exceptional session in this House in response, logically, to the exceptional situation in our country. I wish that none of this was necessary, and yet, in the strangeness that all of this has produced for us these days, there is a fact that I would like to underline in order to conclude, and it is a fact that seems to me indisputable, because, despite certain opinions and attitudes expressed here, this extension of the decree of the state of alarm must be the manifestation of the unity and loyalty of Congress for its Government and the Government for its Congress and for all the Spaniards. Although some feel uncomfortable in this framework of unity - it is obvious - and even yearn for the old confrontation, it is clear that we are only going to defeat the virus, united not since the imposition - I think that we have also been clear, we are going to provide all the necessary information and we are doing so in addition with all the parliamentary groups, not only me, but the Minister of Health himself over these long months that we have been managing the coronavirus from different areas; therefore, not from the imposition - but from the conviction that provides the knowledge of reality, the calm debate and above all the love for our country, for our citizenship and for its health and well-being. That is a passion shared by each of the members of this House, I have no doubt, and for once, ladies and gentlemen, we all pursue the same, exactly the same, here we are not rivals or adversaries, but public representatives united in front of an invisible enemy that we must overcome, which we are obliged to overcome. There are debates, in any case, that do not help. There are others that help us, that enrich us, that open up new perspectives and that make us contemplate other possibilities, and there are other debates that burden us, that confuse us, that delay us, that are the debates that we cannot consent to, even giving the data. I will give a given, I have said it before, in my first intervention, in the replica and now in the duplicate. Now we all have to be supportive of the Madrid Community, and we are, and the Government is precisely articulating all those solidarity mechanisms that we are asking for from different territories, but we have to give the certain figures because, if not, we confuse the citizenry at a time when there is a lot of anguish and a lot of gloomy. The Community of Madrid was given more than one hundred thousand masks yesterday, and from 10 March to date the Community of Madrid has been given 26,8 % of the total number of masks distributed throughout the national territory, i.e. 1,865,468 masks. Therefore, let us contribute to constructive, reliable debates, to give certainty and clarity to the debate and also to respond to the unknowns of the whole citizenry. Therefore, the alternative, in my opinion, is simple: we can waste time and energy on reproaching each other for what no one knew in time, neither you nor Spain nor the world, or we can devote every second of our lives and every gram of our forces to getting out of this situation. And get out together. This is the dilemma to which every political leader in this country has to respond. Ladies and gentlemen, I have no doubt, I know what the answer will be. We are allies in the same line; every public representative, regardless of the party, is useful, indispensable and necessary for this Government. I think I've also exercised it in the first person. I have spoken to all the parliamentary groups, from the broadest to the smallest, and in the same way that we have you that no one has the Government to engage in political confrontation in this situation. Nothing or anyone is going to separate us from the line of unity and loyalty in which we believe and which will lead us to total victory in front of the coronavirus. Therefore, let us not waste time thinking from intuition, let us not run after miraculous solutions that would only serve to accentuate suffering. Let us, therefore, live up to our obligations; it is time to take real leadership with courage, effort and determination. Let's keep our heads cold, our hearts warm and our spirits unbreakable. The head is cold, ladies and gentlemen, because it is very important at a time such as the present one to make decisions, not to act from fear, to correct the diagnoses and the consequences of the measures being taken and which can be taken. To listen to proposals, no doubt, even if they are from our political rivals. Also for the role of each and every one of us in the unity of action that citizens demand of us. To share from loyalty a battle and propose from intelligence and knowledge new weapons to win it. To seek, in short, ladies and gentlemen, the common good. That's what we need the cold head for. Also the warm heart to feel as own the pain of each person. And believe me, I feel like my own. To put us in his place. To be permanently with all Spanish and Spanish. To feel the pain of everyone as their own. To suffer together. To seek the common benefit above one's own. So as not to leave anyone behind, neither now nor after, when we get out of this very unfortunate situation. A firm spirit too, ladies and gentlemen, so as not to doubt. Let's not lose a second, let's have confidence in what we're doing together because we're doing it right, because we're going in the right direction. If we keep the mood firm, the heart warm and the head cold we will bend the curve and eliminate the virus. So let us not look elsewhere, let us not allow doubts to change the direction of our march at this decisive, decisive moment. Let's not give in to fear, to scum. Let's not let small thoughts alter our decision. We come, ladies and gentlemen - I have said this over this weekend - from dark, uncertain days and we are going to spend more dark and uncertain days, but we know where the exit is. We're going to get through this. We just have to step up, moving forward together this week that we said it was going to be very difficult and continue to trust this common response that we are renewing today to recover the most beautiful of our lives. To leave behind - just now, ladies and gentlemen, that spring has begun - this unexpected winter time. To leave it behind united, ladies and gentlemen, always united. Thank you for your support.
